_source line 3121 · 2026-05-20T16:44:33.586Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - Continue Research Director role for `contrastive_trace_safety` targeting EMNLP 2026 via ARR May 2026 (DDL 2026-05-25)
   - Fix literature_map.yaml format so `/distill` works, then trigger distill
   - Use distill results to improve paper positioning and related work
   - Fix user-reported bugs: appendix table-text overlap, Table 38 "i.001" rendering error
   - Submit paper to C群 (Review Group) for formal review
   - Process C群 review report (Overall 3.0 Findings) — build Action Matrix, dispatch fixes
   - Clean up GPU monitoring (remove servers not allocated to project)
   - User requested: visual/layout self-check of current PDF ("目前排版上还有什么问题，先自查一遍")

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026 via ARR: 8-page main text limit, unlimited appendix/references, Limitations doesn't count toward 8 pages
   - Literature map distill system expects dict format for `papers:` key (not YAML list), plus `meta:` and `categories:` fields
   - `_read_map` in `core/tools/literature.py` (line 103-127): reads YAML, sets defaults for meta/categories/papers
   - `distill_papers` reads papers via `lit_map.get("papers")` — needs dict keyed by paper ID
   - LaTeX text-mode `<` in T1 encoding renders as `i`-like character — must use math mode `$<

   - Float specifier `[h]` can cause table-text overlap when combined with `\resizebox`; `[htbp]` allows LaTeX auto-placement
   - ACL template `acl.sty` L190: `\renewcommand\cite{\citep}` — plain `\cite` already maps to `\citep`
   - Crossing rate formula: CR(ℓ) = N⁻¹Σᵢ 𝟙[FCPᵢ^(ℓ) exists]
   - Review scoring: 5=Award, 4=Conference, 3.5=Borderline, 3=Findings, 2.5=Borderline Findings
   - Two-stage statistical design: upstream exploration (nested CV/split-half) → downstream confirmation (HB on 12 final tests)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/literature_map.yaml`
     - Rewrote from YAML list format to dict format with `meta:`, `categories: []`, `papers:` (dict keyed by paper ID)
     - 10 papers: zou2023representation, zhou2024alignment, arditi2024refusal, harmthoughts, safethink, chen2025unfaithful, hewitt2019control, kaya2019shallow, zhou2020bert, zou2023universal
     - Each entry has: title, authors (list), venue, year, arxiv_id, role, verified, bibtex_key, category, relevance, abstract

   - `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/config/resources.yaml`
     - Removed autodl-rtx6000, bjb1-42106, home-1x5090 server entries
     - Only cqa1-21977 remains

   - `docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex`
     - Commit 6243103: Added temporal gap statement ("no prior work tracks how safety signals evolve step-by-step...")
     - Commit 64710f9: Added Chen motivation bridge sentence ("chain-of-thought faithfulness is limited ~25%...")
     - Paper Agent working on: N1 search space quantification, N2+N4 Abstract rewrite, W4 retrospective scope, W5 terminology, W6 a priori prediction

   - `docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex`
     - Commit 6243103: Expanded Zhou ACL'24 citation (pre-training origin + commitment transitions distinction)
     - Commit a4313e8: Added Kaya/PABEE provenance ("identify 'overthinking'... formalize patience-based early exit. We repurpose this for safety probe layer selection")
     - Agent working on: W7 Chan & Yong expansion + 3 new references (SafeProbing, Probing Latent Subspaces, Concrete Roadmap)

   - `docs/paper/sections/method.tex`
     - Commit a4313e8: Added CR inline formula and FCP definition
     - Commit 77b587f: "safety reasoning" → "safety deliberation" terminology fix

   - `docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex`
     - Commit 64710f9: 34 tabular environments wrapped in `\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{...}`
     - Commit 44db1aa: 38 float specifiers `[h]` → `[htbp]`
     - Commit 77b587f: Added 4 missing TOC entries (app:cp_perturbation, app:random_projection, app:e5_mistral, app:hyperparameter_robustness)
     - Commit f268641: Fixed Table 38 "i.001" bug (4 instances `({<}.001)` → `($<$.001)`), label `tab:ba_layer_comparison` → `app:ba_layer_comparison`

   - `docs/paper/references.bib`
     - Agent working on: adding 3 new bib entries (SafeProbing/Zhao 2026, Probing Latent Subspaces/Chia 2025, Concrete Roadmap/Schulz 2025), fixing geiger2025causal year

   - `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`
     - Created with all R22 issues tracked: N1-N5, W1-W8, M2/M5/M6/M8, REF
     - N5 marked `wont_fix` (false alarm), all others `in_progress`

   - `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/ROADMAP.md`
     - Updated to reflect final state: all pre-review tasks checked off, C群 review tasks added

   - Distilled skills (read-only, generated by distill):
     - `skills/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/distilled-research.skill.md` — 4 research gaps, competitor claims, method consensus
     - `skills/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/distilled-writing.skill.md` — narrative patterns, figure design, naming techniques

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Literature map distill failure (3 times)**:

        "content": "---\nname: contrastive-trace-safety-writing\ndescription: LLM safety interpretability 论文叙事结构、图表设计共识与可模仿策略——8 篇深读蒸馏\ntype: writing\nsource_papers: 8\ndistill_date: 2026-05-20\n---\n\n## 叙事结构\n\n### 故事类型：突破局限型主导（6/8，高）\n现有方法看似有效→揭示隐藏缺陷→新视角。Chen 先承认推理模型\"更忠实\"再揭示仅 25%，先正面再转折避免危言耸听。Hewitt 逐条推翻假设（MLP 好？不行；dropout 有用？没用）。**策略：先建立对手最强论据再拆解。**\n\n### 开篇钩子三模式\n1. **许诺-颠覆**（3/8）：Chen \"CoT promises safety... but?\" / Hewitt \"probing achieves high accuracy... but meaningful?\" → 适用于我们：隐状态分析被寄望揭示真实推理，但与文本一致吗？\n2. **对立张力**（3/8）：Zhou alignment vs jailbreak / Arditi helpfulness vs harmlessness\n3. **人类类比命名**（2/8）：Kaya \"overthinking\" / PABEE \"patience\"——命名即贡献\n\n### Intro 4-5 段是主流（5/8）\nP1=hook → P2=现有局限 → P3=方法 → P4=发现 → P5=贡献列表。贡献声明：编号列表（3/8）vs 叙事渐进无显式列表（3/8）并存。\n\n## 图表设计共识\n\n### Figure 1 模式\nmethod_overview（3/8：Zhou/Kaya/PABEE）、teaser_result（2/8：Arditi/Chen）、motivation 实例（2/8：HarmThoughts/Hewitt）。设计共识：对比布局 5/8，颜色编码安全状态 3/8。**Figure 1 必须不看正文即传达核心论点。**\n\n### 结果可视化\n- **折线图**（6/8）：layer-wise 分析、参数扫描首选\n- **分组柱状图**（4/8）：方法间对比。增益突出：仅标本方法数值（SafeThink）、Pareto 前沿（PABEE）、视觉骤降（Arditi 0.9→0.0）\n- **热力图**（2/8）：密集 layer×token 可视化\n- 消融用双面板解耦指标最有效（Hewitt accuracy+selectivity 上下行；Chen 6-panel 网格）\n\n### 表格编排（6/8，高）\n行=methods，by_category 分块（7/8）；bold_best 与无高亮各 3/8；Baseline 3-14 个（中位 ~7）；单次运行为主（5/8）。**full_numbers 绝对主导**（7/8），delta_from_base 未见。\n\n### 图表分工\n趋势→图，多方法×多任务→表，定性例子→图。附录（5/8）放逐模型细分和方法论验证。\n\n## 可模仿技巧\n\n1. **命名即贡献**（3/8，高）：给核心概念起直觉名字（\"overthinking\"/\"patience\"/\"Weak-to-Strong Explanation\"），一词概括工作\n2. **渐进揭示**（3/8，中）：每节独立叙事弧层层推进。Zhou 3.1→3.2→4；Chen 正面→中性→警报\n3. **简洁即论据**（2/8，中）：Arditi 反复强调\"rank-1、<$5、无需训练\"——方法简单时简单性本身是最强修辞\n4. **平衡裁决**（2/8，中）：安全领域先承认进步再揭示不足，比单纯报警更可信（Chen）\n",
        "numLines": 45,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 45
      }

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/skills/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/distilled-research.skill.md",
        "content": "---\nname: contrastive-trace-safety-research\ndescription: LLM safety mechanistic interpretability 领域实验设计共识、方法论趋势与研究空白——10 篇论文蒸馏\ntype: research\nsource_papers: 10\ndistill_date: 2026-05-20\n---\n\n## 方法论主流\n\n### 对比对→方向提取（4/10，高）\n构造 harmful/harmless 对比对，difference-in-means 提取方向。Arditi 128 对提取 refusal direction；RepE 对比刺激做表征读取；HarmThoughts 验证 16 行为类别近正交可分。共识：128-500 对即够。\n\n### 简单探针优于复杂分类器（4/10，高）\nZhou (ACL'24) 用 sklearn 默认 SVM/MLP 达 >95%，\"弱分类器也能分\"是核心论据。Hewitt (EMNLP'19) 证明 MLP selectivity 仅 4.5 vs 线性 26.0。HarmThoughts Linear Probe (~0.49 F1) 优于 RFM/DoM。**经验：线性探针 + 随机标签对照（selectivity）是硬要求。**\n\n### 逐层分析是标配（8/8，高）\nZhou 发现伦理分类前几层即 >95%；Arditi 发现 refusal direction 在 40-80% 深度最强；PABEE 跨层一致性做 early exit。共识：安全信号在不同层有不同功能（早层分类→中层关联→深层生成）。\n\n## 实验设计共识\n\n**指标**：ASR（4/10，安全标准）、分类准确率/Macro-F1（探针评估）、Selectivity（探针质量金标准，Hewitt）、跨层一致性（confusion/patience → crossing rate）\n\n**数据集**：AdvBench（3/10）、JailbreakBench（2/10）、HarmBench（2/10）；推理安全用 HarmThoughts（56,931 句）；Llama 系列最常用（4/10）\n\n**统计**：单次运行为主（5/8），领域对统计显著性要求不高但 Arditi 用了标准误差条\n\n## 竞品 Claim 格局\n\n- 安全信号预训练已存在，对齐仅建立\"桥接关联\"（Zhou ACL'24）\n- Refusal 由单一方向调控，rank-1 编辑即可绕过（Arditi NeurIPS'24）\n- CoT 忠实度仅 ~25%，RL 后趋平（Chen, Anthropic）\n- 安全输出可掩盖不安全推理过程（HarmThoughts）\n\n## 研究空白（高）\n\n1. **隐状态×推理模型**：Zhou 做隐状态不涉及推理模型；HarmThoughts 做推理链仅句子级；Chen 做推理模型仅文本忠实度→无人将隐状态探针用于推理安全轨迹\n2. **安全信号时序动态**：所有论文做 layer-wise（空间），无人追踪 token 生成过程中的安全信号演变（时间）\n3. **跨层一致性用于安全选层**：Kaya/PABEE 建立理论仅用于效率，未用于安全探针最优层选择\n4. **控制任务用于安全探针**：Hewitt selectivity 仅应用于语言任务，未在安全分类验证\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 41
      }

The text-HS contrast is therefore metric-dependent rather than a blanket modality difference.

_source line 3179 · 2026-05-20T16:46:24.259Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The issue is clear now. The paper uses `\cref` everywhere, but `\cref` produces lowercase "table 1", "figure 1" etc. by default, while `\Cref` produces "Table 1", "Figure 1". 

When `\cref` is at the start of a sentence, it should be `\Cref` to get proper capitalization.

The appendix TOC (lines 4-30) uses `\cref` for listing items — those are fine since they're not sentence starters per se, they're list items.


I need to check how cleveref is configured in the paper. The `capitalise` option would make `\cref` always produce capitalized versions, but without it, lowercase is the default. Let me look at the package configuration to understand what's actually happening.
```

</details>

### Assistant

_source line 3185 · 2026-05-20T16:46:43.221Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
`\usepackage{cleveref}` without the `capitalise` option means `\cref` produces lowercase names. The ACL/EMNLP convention requires capitalized cross-references when they serve as a noun phrase (e.g., "Table 1 shows..." should be "Table 1", not "table 1").

There are two approaches:
1. Add `capitalise` option: `\usepackage[capitalise]{cleveref}` — this makes all `\cref` produce "Table", "Figure", "Section" etc.
2. Change sentence-start `\cref` to `\Cref`
 
The standard fix for ACL/EMNLP papers is adding the `capitalise` option to cleveref, which ensures all references are properly capitalized as required by the style guide.

For the appendix with 20 pages and 40 tables, a table of contents is helpful for navigation and EMNLP doesn't restrict appendix content, so I'll recommend keeping it while giving the user the choice.

I can make the cleveref change directly since it's just a one-line modification in the main configuration file, then handle the remaining formatting questions.
```

</details>

### Tool result

_source line 3216 · 2026-05-20T16:48:36.493Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012ELXEhA1egqg6cAm4bu4Rp`</summary>

```text
1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +15.4 to +21.9pp against a 384-dimensional distilled sentence encoder (MiniLM-L6-v2; Holm-Bonferroni corrected; upper bound), but this advantage is doubly conditional: it diminishes with stronger text encoders (4/4 HB at 384d $\to$ 1/4 at 1024d BGE-large) and partially overlaps with temporal position (3/4 models retain significant non-positional advantage under linear residualization; 2/4 under quadratic control). The 7--8B results are validated via nested 5-fold CV: precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness; R1-32B shows a pattern consistent with position mediation and falls below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2	
```

Let me fix this by integrating the dagger explanation inline instead of using a footnote marker. The current text says:


"The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$)"

          "+Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +15.4 to +21.9pp against a 384-dimensional distilled sentence encoder (MiniLM-L6-v2; Holm-Bonferroni corrected; upper bound), but this advantage is doubly conditional: it diminishes with stronger text encoders (4/4 HB at 384d $\\to$ 1/4 at 1024d BGE-large) and partially overlaps with temporal position (3/4 models retain significant non-positional advantage under linear residualization; 2/4 under quadratic control). The 7--8B results are validated via nested 5-fold CV: precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory (sample-limited, $n \\leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness; R1-32B shows a pattern consistent with position mediation and falls below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \\leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "userModified": false,
    "replaceAll": false
  },

The $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Had we reported only CR-composite-layer results, we would have concluded a scale-dependent HS advantage that is entirely a methodological artifact. A random-label control on QwQ-32B confirms that the crossing-rate shallow-layer bias is a geometric property of the representations (100\\% of random-label repeats select shallow layers), while threshold-based deep-layer selection requires real safety labels (\\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \\cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.\\looseness=-1",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The `figure*` approach caused both figures to float to pages 31-32 (end of the document, in the appendix area). The main text pages 6-8 no longer have any figures — they're all text. This is worse than before.

The problem is that `figure*` in two-column layout defers to the top of the next page, and if there's no room, it keeps floating. Since the main text is very dense, the figures float all the way to the end.

I need to revert this approach. Instead, I should keep the `figure` (single column) environment but perhaps the user's concern was about the figure content being small (the actual plot elements), not the figure width in the layout. 

Looking back at the original layout, the figures were positioned on pages 6 and 8 at column width, which is actually standard for two-column ACL papers. The real issue is probably that the figure elements themselves—labels, lines, tick marks—need to be larger and more readable. I should revert to single-column `figure` and instead ask the Agent to regenerate the figures with better visual clarity when they handle the M2/M6 fixes.

But Figure 1 is a 2×2 heatmap grid, which is genuinely too complex to fit readably in a single column. I could try `figure*` with placement overrides like `[!htbp]` or `[!t]`, but in two-column mode, `figure*` floats are constrained to the top of pages, which makes placement fragile. The practical solution is to use `figure*` for Figure 1 since it actually needs the full width to display the heatmap grid properly.

For Figure 2, the forest plot is simpler and might work at column width with larger fonts, but I could also make it `figure*` if needed. I'll keep both as `figure*` for now — placing Figure 1's environment at the end of the Method section so it floats to the top of the Experiments page, and Figure 2 earlier in Experiments to appear on the following page.

Actually, the simplest fix is to revert the `figure*` changes and instead have the Agent regenerate both figures with larger, more readable elements when working on M2/M6. That avoids wrestling with LaTeX float placement and directly addresses the user's concern about the figures being too small.
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{cross-validated}\footnote{In our usage, \emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \emph{Exploratory} ($\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.\looseness=-1
10	
11	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers across all four models: L0 for R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep; \cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}) and OT-7B, L2 (3\%) for QwQ-32B, and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same deep layer---L63 (98\%) for both 32B models---consistent with their shared structure, though not mathematically guaranteed. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20; \cref{app:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).\looseness=-1
14	
15	\begin{table}[t]
16	\centering
17	\small
18	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
19	\toprule
20	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{CR-comp.}$^a$ & \textbf{Thresh.}$^b$ & $p$ \textbf{(thresh.)} \\
21	\midrule
22	R1-8B & L0 (0\%) & L14 (44\%) & ${<}0.001$ \\
23	OT-7B & L0 (0\%) & L16 (57\%) & ${<}0.001$ \\
24	QwQ-32B & L2 (3\%) & L63 (98\%) & ${<}0.005$ \\
25	R1-32B & L10 (16\%) & L63 (98\%) & ${<}0.001$ \\
26	\bottomrule
27	\end{tabular}
28	\caption{Layer selection sensitivity. $^a$\,Crossing-rate composite (bal\_acc $\times$ crossing\_rate). $^b$\,Consensus of threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite). Rightmost: HS vs.\ text precision $p$-value at threshold-based layer (e.g., QwQ-32B CR-composite layer: bal\_acc $p{=}0.35$, precision $p{=}0.83$, both non-significant).}
29	\label{tab:layer_selection}
30	\end{table}
31	
32	\begin{figure*}[t]
33	\centering
34	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig_layer_heatmap.pdf}
35	\caption{Per-layer metric heatmap (2$\times$2). Each column: one of five metrics (min-max normalized). Blue dashed: CR-composite layer (bal\_acc $\times$ crossing\_rate); orange dash-dot: threshold-based layer (consensus of threshold-based metrics). For R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep), crossing rate drives CR-composite selection to L0 while threshold-based metrics retain L14. At 32B, CR-composite selections cluster near input layers while threshold-based metrics peak at deep layers.}
36	\label{fig:layer_heatmap}
37	\end{figure*}
38	
39	The downstream impact is severe: at CR-composite layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. At threshold-based layers, the HS advantage is graded: cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B, R1-8B; nested CV), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (residualized gap below MDE). The $\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Had we reported only CR-composite-layer results, we would have concluded a scale-dependent HS advantage that is entirely a methodological artifact. A random-label control on QwQ-32B confirms that the crossing-rate shallow-layer bias is a geometric property of the representations (100\% of random-label repeats select shallow layers), while threshold-based deep-layer selection requires real safety labels (\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.\looseness=-1
40

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017chgbw4AFRj2biZAUd6Q4K`</summary>

```text
45	We evaluate seven alternatives to contextualize probe performance (\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD~\citep{adams2007bayesian} and CUSUM~\citep{page1954cusum} for change-point detection; Mahalanobis distance following TrajGuard~\citep{trajguard}; RepE mean-difference directions~\citep{zou2023representation}; two-layer MLP probes; CCS-adapted~\citep{burns2023discovering}; and cross-model transfer via PCA. A position-only baseline ($t/T$) controls for temporal artifacts. Detailed configurations in \cref{app:bocpd_config,app:transfer_details}.
46	
47	\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}
48	\label{sec:evaluation}
49	
50	We report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR, coverage, and lead time. We exclude F1 (conflates precision and recall), AUROC (threshold-free, cannot diagnose layer selection bias), and MCC (redundant with BA under balanced priors). Positive classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed across all models as the value balancing coverage ($\geq$70\%) and safe-trace false positive rate ($\leq$50\%); \cref{tab:k_ablation} confirms that the HS vs.\ text conclusion direction is consistent at $K{=}3$, $5$, and $7$, with coverage and FPR trading off monotonically. Modality comparisons use BCa bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples, resampling traces as clusters: each trace is an independent unit and all its steps are included or excluded together, preserving within-trace dependence)~\citep{efron1993bootstrap,diciccio1996bootstrap} with Holm-Bonferroni step-down (3 metrics $\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\alpha{=}0.05$); the 4-test framework yields identical conclusions (\cref{app:holm_bonferroni}). For 32B models ($n \leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes (BCa assumes approximate pivotality; heavy-tailed score distributions could produce anti-conservative intervals). Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\cref{app:control}). $\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).
51	
52	\paragraph{Hyperparameter search space.} The full exploration spans ${\sim}$5 metrics $\times$ 7 windows $\times$ 28--64 layers $\approx$ 980--2{,}240 configurations per model. A two-stage design protects against selection bias: upstream layer/window exploration uses nested CV and split-half validation at 7--8B (\cref{app:nested_cv,app:split_half}); downstream modality tests apply HB to 12 final comparisons. The 32B models lack nested CV (${\sim}$4 traces per fold) and are marked exploratory ($\dagger$).\looseness=-1
53	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01FSihZ2UxJRagb1hkujA6h5`</summary>

```text
50	We report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR, coverage, and lead time. We exclude F1 (conflates precision and recall), AUROC (threshold-free, cannot diagnose layer selection bias), and MCC (redundant with BA under balanced priors). Positive classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed across all models as the value balancing coverage ($\geq$70\%) and safe-trace false positive rate ($\leq$50\%); \cref{tab:k_ablation} confirms that the HS vs.\ text conclusion direction is consistent at $K{=}3$, $5$, and $7$, with coverage and FPR trading off monotonically. Modality comparisons use BCa bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples, resampling traces as clusters: each trace is an independent unit and all its steps are included or excluded together, preserving within-trace dependence)~\citep{efron1993bootstrap,diciccio1996bootstrap} with Holm-Bonferroni step-down (3 metrics $\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\alpha{=}0.05$); the 4-test framework yields identical conclusions (\cref{app:holm_bonferroni}). For 32B models ($n \leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes (BCa assumes approximate pivotality; heavy-tailed score distributions could produce anti-conservative intervals). Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\cref{app:control}). $\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).
51	
52	\paragraph{Hyperparameter search space.} The full exploration spans ${\sim}$5 metrics $\times$ 7 windows $\times$ 28--64 layers $\approx$ 980--2{,}240 configurations per model. A two-stage design protects against selection bias: upstream layer/window exploration uses nested CV and split-half validation at 7--8B (\cref{app:nested_cv,app:split_half}); downstream modality tests apply HB to 12 final comparisons. The 32B models lack nested CV (${\sim}$4 traces per fold) and are marked exploratory ($\dagger$).\looseness=-1
53	
```

Control task selectivity~\\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\\cref{app:control}). $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameter search space.} The full exploration spans ${\\sim}$5 metrics $\\times$ 7 windows $\\times$ 28--64 layers $\\approx$ 980--2{,}240 configurations per model. A two-stage design protects against selection bias: upstream layer/window exploration uses nested CV and split-half validation at 7--8B (\\cref{app:nested_cv,app:split_half}); downstream modality tests apply HB to 12 final comparisons. The 32B models lack nested CV (${\\sim}$4 traces per fold) and are marked exploratory ($\\dagger$).\\looseness=-1\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Problem Setup and Methods}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}\n\\label{sec:commitment-point}\n\nWhen a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition. This binary formulation is a deliberate simplification; we discuss its limits in \\cref{sec:limitations}. Steps before CP are \\emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \\emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The classification task produces a score $s_t \\in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.\n\nWe study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\\cref{tab:models}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Arch.} & \\textbf{Params} & \\textbf{Layers} & \\textbf{Hidden} & \\textbf{$n$ (total\\,/\\,test)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & Llama & 8B & 32 & 4096 & 209\\,/\\,43 \\\\\nOT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\\,/\\,47 \\\\\nR1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\\,/\\,20 \\\\\nQwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\\,/\\,19 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Models studied. R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}\n\\label{tab:models}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Probe Architecture}\n\\label{sec:probe-architecture}\n\n\\paragraph{Hidden-state (HS) probes.} At each step $t$, we extract the last-token hidden state $\\mathbf{h}_t^{(\\ell)} \\in \\mathbb{R}^d$ from layer $\\ell$ and train an L2-regularized logistic regression classifier (balanced class weights, $C{=}1.0$, LBFGS, max\\_iter${}=2000$) on binary CP labels. Sweeping $C \\in [0.01, 100]$ yields $<$2pp BA variation (\\cref{sec:experiments}), confirming robustness to this default. Layer selection is analyzed in \\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}.\n\n\\paragraph{Text probes.} Each step's text is encoded with all-MiniLM-L6-v2~\\citep{wang2020minilm} ($\\mathbb{R}^{384}$), a distilled sentence encoder from the Sentence-BERT family~\\citep{reimers2019sentence}. The same logistic regression is applied as a surface-level modality baseline.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal windowing.} For window size $W$, we concatenate features from steps $[t{-}W{+}1, \\ldots, t]$. We evaluate $W \\in \\{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\\}$ and select per-model on validation. GRU sequence models are also evaluated under two protocols: a 70/30 train/test split (primary) and the same 60/20/20 split used for LR experiments (for fair comparison; \\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds,app:gru_details}).\n\n\\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}\n\\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}\n\nLayer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate formalizes cross-layer prediction disagreement~\\citep{kaya2019shallow,zhou2020bert}, and additive composite. A \\emph{first crossing point} (FCP) exists for trace~$i$ at layer~$\\ell$ when the probe score $s_{t'}^{(\\ell)} > \\tau$ for $K$ consecutive steps; the crossing rate is $\\mathrm{CR}(\\ell) = N^{-1}\\!\\sum_{i} \\mathbb{1}[\\mathrm{FCP}_i^{(\\ell)} \\text{ exists}]$. A multiplicative composite (BA $\\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \\emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline. Metrics at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \\emph{threshold-based} selection. This taxonomy is empirical, but cosine compression at shallow layers predicts a priori that any threshold-crossing metric exhibits shallow-layer bias---confirmed by results (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). Crossing rate inflates where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\\times$ smaller; \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Threshold-based metrics share a confusion matrix and empirically converge; BA independently confirms. Results in \\cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \\cref{app:layer_sweep}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{Baseline Methods}\n\\label{sec:baselines}\n\nWe evaluate seven alternatives to contextualize probe performance (\\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD~\\citep{adams2007bayesian} and CUSUM~\\citep{page1954cusum} for change-point detection; Mahalanobis distance following TrajGuard~\\citep{trajguard}; RepE mean-difference directions~\\citep{zou2023representation}; two-layer MLP probes; CCS-adapted~\\citep{burns2023discovering}; and cross-model transfer via PCA. A position-only baseline ($t/T$) controls for temporal artifacts. Detailed configurations in \\cref{app:bocpd_config,app:transfer_details}.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}\n\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\nWe report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR, coverage, and lead time. We exclude F1 (conflates precision and recall), AUROC (threshold-free, cannot diagnose layer selection bias), and MCC (redundant with BA under balanced priors). Positive classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed across all models as the value balancing coverage ($\\geq$70\\%) and safe-trace false positive rate ($\\leq$50\\%); \\cref{tab:k_ablation} confirms that the HS vs.\\ text conclusion direction is consistent at $K{=}3$, $5$, and $7$, with coverage and FPR trading off monotonically. Modality comparisons use BCa bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples, resampling traces as clusters: each trace is an independent unit and all its steps are included or excluded together, preserving within-trace dependence)~\\citep{efron1993bootstrap,diciccio1996bootstrap} with Holm-Bonferroni step-down (3 metrics $\\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\\alpha{=}0.05$); the 4-test framework yields identical conclusions (\\cref{app:holm_bonferroni}). For 32B models ($n \\leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes (BCa assumes approximate pivotality; heavy-tailed score distributions could produce anti-conservative intervals). Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\\cref{app:control}). $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameter search space.} The full exploration spans ${\\sim}$5 metrics $\\times$ 7 windows $\\times$ 28--64 layers $\\approx$ 980--2{,}240 configurations per model. A two-stage design protects against selection bias: upstream layer/window exploration uses nested CV and split-half validation at 7--8B (\\cref{app:nested_cv,app:split_half}); downstream modality tests apply HB to 12 final comparisons. The 32B models lack nested CV (${\\sim}$4 traces per fold) and are marked exploratory ($\\dagger$).\\looseness=-1\n",

Still floating to p31-32. The `figure*` in two-column mode is notoriously hard to control. The problem is that the main text is so dense (8 pages packed) that there's no room for a full-width figure.

Let me rethink this completely. The issue is fundamental to ACL two-column layout: `figure*` will always float to a later page top if the current page can't fit it. With 8 tightly packed pages, there's no room.

Better approach: revert everything back to `figure` (single column) for both, and instead have the Agent regenerate the figures with larger internal elements (larger fonts, thicker lines, more padding). 

The real constraint here is that column-width figures in a two-column layout are inherently limited in space—about 45% of page width—which makes a 2×2 heatmap feel cramped. The `figure*` environment would solve this but keeps floating away. I could try forcing it with placement flags or specialized packages like `stfloats` or `dblfloatfix` that give better control over full-width figure positioning, but the simplest fix might be to stick with single-column figures and just make the content inside them more readable. I see the real issue now — the user is complaining about the figure content being too small, not the LaTeX dimensions. I should revert both figures back to column width and have the Agent regenerate them with larger, more readable internal elements instead.

    "oldString": "\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig_layer_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-layer metric heatmap (2$\\times$2). Each column: one of five metrics (min-max normalized). Blue dashed: CR-composite layer (bal\\_acc $\\times$ crossing\\_rate); orange dash-dot: threshold-based layer (consensus of threshold-based metrics). For R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep), crossing rate drives CR-composite selection to L0 while threshold-based metrics retain L14. At 32B, CR-composite selections cluster near input layers while threshold-based metrics peak at deep layers.}\n\\label{fig:layer_heatmap}\n\\end{figure*}",
    "newString": "",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Problem Setup and Methods}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}\n\\label{sec:commitment-point}\n\nWhen a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition. This binary formulation is a deliberate simplification; we discuss its limits in \\cref{sec:limitations}. Steps before CP are \\emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \\emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The classification task produces a score $s_t \\in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.\n\nWe study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\\cref{tab:models}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Arch.} & \\textbf{Params} & \\textbf{Layers} & \\textbf{Hidden} & \\textbf{$n$ (total\\,/\\,test)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & Llama & 8B & 32 & 4096 & 209\\,/\\,43 \\\\\nOT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\\,/\\,47 \\\\\nR1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\\,/\\,20 \\\\\nQwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\\,/\\,19 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Models studied. R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}\n\\label{tab:models}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Probe Architecture}\n\\label{sec:probe-architecture}\n\n\\paragraph{Hidden-state (HS) probes.} At each step $t$, we extract the last-token hidden state $\\mathbf{h}_t^{(\\ell)} \\in \\mathbb{R}^d$ from layer $\\ell$ and train an L2-regularized logistic regression classifier (balanced class weights, $C{=}1.0$, LBFGS, max\\_iter${}=2000$) on binary CP labels. Sweeping $C \\in [0.01, 100]$ yields $<$2pp BA variation (\\cref{sec:experiments}), confirming robustness to this default. Layer selection is analyzed in \\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}.\n\n\\paragraph{Text probes.} Each step's text is encoded with all-MiniLM-L6-v2~\\citep{wang2020minilm} ($\\mathbb{R}^{384}$), a distilled sentence encoder from the Sentence-BERT family~\\citep{reimers2019sentence}. The same logistic regression is applied as a surface-level modality baseline.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal windowing.} For window size $W$, we concatenate features from steps $[t{-}W{+}1, \\ldots, t]$. We evaluate $W \\in \\{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\\}$ and select per-model on validation. GRU sequence models are also evaluated under two protocols: a 70/30 train/test split (primary) and the same 60/20/20 split used for LR experiments (for fair comparison; \\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds,app:gru_details}).\n\n\\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}\n\\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}\n\nLayer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate formalizes cross-layer prediction disagreement~\\citep{kaya2019shallow,zhou2020bert}, and additive composite. A \\emph{first crossing point} (FCP) exists for trace~$i$ at layer~$\\ell$ when the probe score $s_{t'}^{(\\ell)} > \\tau$ for $K$ consecutive steps; the crossing rate is $\\mathrm{CR}(\\ell) = N^{-1}\\!\\sum_{i} \\mathbb{1}[\\mathrm{FCP}_i^{(\\ell)} \\text{ exists}]$. A multiplicative composite (BA $\\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \\emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline. Metrics at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \\emph{threshold-based} selection. This taxonomy is empirical, but cosine compression at shallow layers predicts a priori that any threshold-crossing metric exhibits shallow-layer bias---confirmed by results (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). Crossing rate inflates where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\\times$ smaller; \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Threshold-based metrics share a confusion matrix and empirically converge; BA independently confirms. Results in \\cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \\cref{app:layer_sweep}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{Baseline Methods}\n\\label{sec:baselines}\n\nWe evaluate seven alternatives to contextualize probe performance (\\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD~\\citep{adams2007bayesian} and CUSUM~\\citep{page1954cusum} for change-point detection; Mahalanobis distance following TrajGuard~\\citep{trajguard}; RepE mean-difference directions~\\citep{zou2023representation}; two-layer MLP probes; CCS-adapted~\\citep{burns2023discovering}; and cross-model transfer via PCA. A position-only baseline ($t/T$) controls for temporal artifacts. Detailed configurations in \\cref{app:bocpd_config,app:transfer_details}.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}\n\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\nWe report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR, coverage, and lead time. We exclude F1 (conflates precision and recall), AUROC (threshold-free, cannot diagnose layer selection bias), and MCC (redundant with BA under balanced priors). Positive classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed across all models as the value balancing coverage ($\\geq$70\\%) and safe-trace false positive rate ($\\leq$50\\%); \\cref{tab:k_ablation} confirms that the HS vs.\\ text conclusion direction is consistent at $K{=}3$, $5$, and $7$, with coverage and FPR trading off monotonically. Modality comparisons use BCa bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples, resampling traces as clusters: each trace is an independent unit and all its steps are included or excluded together, preserving within-trace dependence)~\\citep{efron1993bootstrap,diciccio1996bootstrap} with Holm-Bonferroni step-down (3 metrics $\\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\\alpha{=}0.05$); the 4-test framework yields identical conclusions (\\cref{app:holm_bonferroni}). For 32B models ($n \\leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes (BCa assumes approximate pivotality; heavy-tailed score distributions could produce anti-conservative intervals). Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\\cref{app:control}). $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameter search space.} The full exploration spans ${\\sim}$5 metrics $\\times$ 7 windows $\\times$ 28--64 layers $\\approx$ 980--2{,}240 configurations per model. A two-stage design protects against selection bias: upstream layer/window exploration uses nested CV and split-half validation at 7--8B (\\cref{app:nested_cv,app:split_half}); downstream modality tests apply HB to 12 final comparisons. The 32B models lack nested CV (${\\sim}$4 traces per fold) and are marked exploratory ($\\dagger$).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig_layer_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-layer metric heatmap (2$\\times$2). Each column: one of five metrics (min-max normalized). Blue dashed: CR-composite layer (bal\\_acc $\\times$ crossing\\_rate); orange dash-dot: threshold-based layer (consensus of threshold-based metrics). For R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep), crossing rate drives CR-composite selection to L0 while threshold-based metrics retain L14. At 32B, CR-composite selections cluster near input layers while threshold-based metrics peak at deep layers.}\n\\label{fig:layer_heatmap}\n\\end{figure*}\n",

30	\end{table}
31	
32	The downstream impact is severe: at CR-composite layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. At threshold-based layers, the HS advantage is graded: cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B, R1-8B; nested CV), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (residualized gap below MDE). The $\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Had we reported only CR-composite-layer results, we would have concluded a scale-dependent HS advantage that is entirely a methodological artifact. A random-label control on QwQ-32B confirms that the crossing-rate shallow-layer bias is a geometric property of the representations (100\% of random-label repeats select shallow layers), while threshold-based deep-layer selection requires real safety labels (\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.\looseness=-1
33	
34	\subsection{HS vs.\ Text Comparison}\label{sec:hs-text}

  "project": "contrastive_trace_safety",
  "message": "**[追加要求] 用户反馈图太小，更新 M2/M6 图表重绘规格：**\n\nACL 双栏布局下 `\\columnwidth` 约 3.3 英寸。`figure*` 全宽在我们 8 页预算下会浮动到文末，不可用。必须在单栏宽度内保证可读性。\n\n**Figure 1 (heatmap) 重绘规格：**\n- figsize 建议 (7, 8) 或更大（matplotlib 会缩放到 columnwidth，大画布 = 更高分辨率）\n- 字号：axis label 14pt, tick label 11pt, title/subtitle 13pt\n- colorbar label 11pt\n- 线宽：dashed/dash-dot 标记线 2.0pt\n- 子图间距：`hspace=0.3, wspace=0.25`\n- 输出 300 dpi PDF\n\n**Figure 2 (forest plot) 重绘规格：**\n- figsize 建议 (7, 4)\n- 字号：axis label 14pt, tick label 11pt, model name label 12pt, legend 11pt\n- marker size 8+, error bar linewidth 1.5\n- 输出 300 dpi PDF\n\n两图都输出到 `docs/paper/figures/` 覆盖原文件。完成后本地编译验证。"
